1.4  Linear Kepler Dynamics & Closed-Form Solutions

Kepler-type dynamics correspond to the case that the central-force potential is given by:

\displaystyle V^{0}\,=\,-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/r\,=\,-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}u\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}\,=\,-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}

(140)

The projective coordinate Hamiltonian,
{{H}}
, is then given by:

\displaystyle{{K}}=\tfrac{1}{2}{\nu}^{2}-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/r\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\boxed{{{H}}=\tfrac{1}{2}u^{2}\big({\ell}^{2}+u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}u}\;\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}\big(u^{2}{\ell}^{2}+w^{2}\big)-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}u

(141)

with
{\ell}^{2}=q^{2}p^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})^{2}
.
The ODEs for perturbed Kepler-type dynamics in the projective coordinates
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
— or the alternative set
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
— are then easily obtained by the substitution
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{u}V^{0}=-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}
wherever it appears in the preceding developments of section
1.2
.
For instance, the
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized dynamics for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)
follow from Eq.(
•
‣
1.2
) as:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\acute{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\\[4.0pt]
\acute{u}\,=\,w&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{w}=-{\ell}^{2}u+{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}+f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\text{\scriptsize{or,}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{u}\,=\,w/{\ell}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{w}=-{\ell}u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}\neq{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\hskip 9.24994pt

where
w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
is a quasi-momenta coordinate used in place of
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}

(see footnote for canonical equations for
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
18
18

18

The perturbed Kepler dynamics for the conjugate pair
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\acute{u}=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[3.0pt]
\acute{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=-\tfrac{1}{u}\big({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)+{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/u^{2}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}\qquad\quad{\text{\footnotesize{or,}}}\qquad\quad\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{u}=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}/{\ell}\\[3.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=-\tfrac{1}{u{\ell}}\big({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)+{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/({\ell}u^{2})+\mathring{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}

In the unperturbed case (
f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=0
), the above is a nonlinear
first
-order system. But, it is indeed equivalent to the linear
second
-order ODEs for
u
in Eq.(

).
).
As noted previously, the particular form of
V^{0}
has no impact whatsoever on the “

Kepler Solutions.

In the following, we consider only the unperturbed Kepler problem — that is,
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}=0=({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
.
Then, the
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized unperturbed Kepler dynamics are as follows
(using
w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{Kepler-type }}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{dynamics}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{array}[]{llllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
\acute{u}\,=\,w&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{w}=-{\ell}^{2}u+{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\text{\scriptsize{or,}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{u}\,=\,w/{\ell}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{w}=-{\ell}u+{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}/{{\ell}}\end{array}}

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\hskip 9.24994pt

As is the case for any cental-force dynamics,
the angular momentum is conserved,
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
, such that the above systems in Eq.(
1.4
) may be regarded as linear systems.
19
19

19

More specifically, they are families of linear ODEs, parameterized by values of the (constant) angular momentum,
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
.

Moreover, it follows from
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
that the parameters
s
and
\tau
defined in Eq.(
69
) are related as follows (assuming
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=s_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
):

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}\tau={\ell}{\mathrm{d}}s

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{central-force}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{dynamics}}}\end{array}\;\Rightarrow\;\;\hskip 9.24994pt{\ell}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\hskip 9.24994pt\tau={\ell}s

such that it makes little difference whether one wishes to express solutions in terms of
s
or
\tau
as reparameterization between them is trivial. For brevity, we often express solutions in terms of
\tau
.
For instance, either system in Eq.(
1.4
) may be solved for solutions in terms of initial conditions and
s
or
\tau={\ell}s
as follows (assuming
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=s_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
for convenience):

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}&\!\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,-\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}&\!\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,-\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u_{\tau}&\!\!\!\!=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
w_{\tau}&\!\!\!\!=\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,-\,{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\tau={\ell}s\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{0}\\[3.0pt]
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}|\!|=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}|\!|\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[3.0pt]
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}|\!|=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}|\!|\end{array}

were
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}
and
{\ell}
really mean
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
and
{\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
.
The above solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau})
were discussed in more detail in section
1.3
.
As noted in Eq.(
124
), these solutions directly verify preservation of
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
,
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}|\!|
,
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
, and
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}|\!|
.

Solution for
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
.
The Kepler solutions for the conjugate pair
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
is easily recovered from Eq.(
1.4
) above using
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=w/u^{2}
.
20
20

20

Specifically,
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=w/u^{2}\leftrightarrow w=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
leads to:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}u_{\tau}\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{0}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\qquad,\qquad p_{u_{\tau}}\,=\,\dfrac{-{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{0}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{\big[(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{1}{{\ell}}u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{0}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big]^{2}}\end{array}

•

Time solution.
The Kepler solution for
t(\tau)

is obtained from that of
u(\tau)
as follows:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\;\;,\;\;{\ell}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}t-t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,=\,\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\Big((u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\Big)^{-2}{\mathrm{d}}\tau\end{array}

•

Second-order dynamics.
Though we focus on the first-order dynamics in Eq.(
1.4
), we note that even
without
simplifying anything with
q=1
or
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
, these are equivalent to the following second-order ODEs for a driven linear harmonic oscillator
[
11
,
15
]
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\!\acute{\vphantom{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}$\hss}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}+{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}=0\\[4.0pt]
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u+{\ell}^{2}u={\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt{\text{\scriptsize{or,}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}$\hss}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}+{{\boldsymbol{q}}}=0\\[4.0pt]
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u+u={\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/{\ell}^{2}\end{array}

•

Simplifications.

Nothing so far has been simplified using the integrals of motion
q=1
or
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
(which also imply
{\ell}\simeq p
). As discussed, we are free to make these simplifications such that, for example, the above already-linear dynamics for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
in Eq.(
1.4
) are, after simplifying using Eq.(
1.1
), equivalent to the following still-linear system:

\displaystyle\;\;\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{array}[]{cc}q=1\\
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0\end{array}$}}\;\Rightarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\left\{\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\simeq{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\simeq-p^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq-{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\end{array}\right.\hskip 18.49988pt{\text{\footnotesize{or,}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\simeq\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\simeq-{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq-p{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\ell}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\simeq p_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=p\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt

The ODEs for
(u,w)
in Eq.(
1.4
), could also be rewritten with
{\ell}\simeq p
. Thus, the Kepler solutions in Eq.(
1.4
), when simplified using the relations in Eq.(
1.1
), are equivalent to:

\displaystyle\;\;\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{array}[]{cc}q=1\\
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0\end{array}$}}\Rightarrow\hskip 9.24994pt\left\{\;\;\boxed{\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\!\!\!\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\!\!\!\simeq\;{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-p_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u_{\tau}=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}&\!\!\!\simeq(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p_{0}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{p_{0}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p_{0}^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
w_{\tau}=\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\!\!\!\simeq\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-p_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p_{0}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}}\right.\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\tau={\ell}s\simeq ps\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{array}

where the above simplified solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau})
again directly verify that
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
is preserved. However, as noted in Eq.(
•
‣
1.3
), they do not directly verify preservation of
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}|\!|
,
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
, or
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}|\!|
; these are preserved
iff
we limit initial conditions to
q_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=1
and
\lambda_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
(which is what the above already assumes), which then leads to
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}|\!|\simeq|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}|\!|\simeq 1
,
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\simeq{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\simeq 0
, and
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}|\!|\simeq|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}|\!|\simeq{\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
.

Cartesian Coordinate Kepler Solutions.

The Kepler solutions for cartesian position and velocity coordinates,
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})
, may now be recovered from the above solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)
using either the full transformation as given in Eq.(
1.1
) or using the simplified relations from Eq.(
1.1
). That is,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{unsimplified}}}\end{array}\!\!:&\hskip 9.24994pt{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u_{\tau}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}\,=\,-u_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}-w_{\tau}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}\;=\,-u_{\tau}\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}-w_{\tau}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}\\
\begin{array}[]{cc}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{simplified with}}}\\
\text{\footnotesize{$q=1,\;{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0$}}\end{array}\!\!:&\hskip 9.24994pt{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}\,\simeq\,\tfrac{1}{u_{\tau}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}\,\simeq\,u_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}-w_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{t}\end{array}

We first consider the unsimplified relations. For the case at hand (Kepler dynamics), these are equivalent to
{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}=\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}\tfrac{1}{u_{\tau}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}
and
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}=-\tfrac{1}{q_{0}}(u_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}+w_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau})
— because
q
is always an integral of motion, and
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
is an integral of motion for any central-force dynamics.
Substituting into these relations the unsimplified Kepler solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},u_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau},w_{\tau})
from Eq.(
1.4
) leads to:

\displaystyle\;\;\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}=\dfrac{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}}{u_{\tau}}\,=\,\dfrac{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}=-u_{\tau}\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}-w_{\tau}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}&\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big[{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)+{\ell}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})\end{array}\;\;

with
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}

the velocity at
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
.
21
21

21

If
\tau=0
coincides with periapsis, then
\tau
is the true anomaly and
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}|\!|\leq|\!|{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}|\!|
.

The above may be written in terms of
({{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
using:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{0}\\[3.0pt]
u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=1/r_{0}\\[3.0pt]
w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-\dot{r}_{0}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{0}={{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[3.0pt]
{\ell}^{2}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{2}=q_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{2}p_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})^{2}=r_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{2}{\nu}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})^{2}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}&\!\!\!\!=\,-u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[3.0pt]
&\!\!\!\!\simeq\,u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{array}

where none of the above require the simplifications
q=1
or
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
.
Eq.(
1.4
) then leads to

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}\,=\,\dfrac{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,-\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{(1/r_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,-\,\frac{1}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\,=\,\dfrac{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{\tau}}{1/r_{\tau}}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{\tau}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big[{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)+{\ell}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})\end{array}

with
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
and
{\ell}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
given in terms of
({{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
as in Eq.(
1.4
).

Simplifications.
Consider the simplified relations in Eq.(
1.4
). Substitution of the simplified Kepler solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},u_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau},w_{\tau})
from Eq.(
•
‣
1.4
) then leads to

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\tau}\simeq\dfrac{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}}{u_{\tau}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,\dfrac{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\end{array}

Relations with the Perifocal Basis.

The close relation between the projective coordinates,
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
, and the LVLH basis was discussed in Eq.(
1.1
)-Eq.(
1.1
).
Let us now consider another set of three mutually orthogonal vectors often encountered in Kepler dynamics; the Laplace-Runge-Lenz (LRL) vector/eccentricity vector,
{{\boldsymbol{e}}}
, and the Hamilton vector,
{{\boldsymbol{h}}}
, along with the usual specific angular momentum
vector,
{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}
.
22
22

22

The Laplace-Runge-Lenz (LRL) vector and the Hamilton vector are often defined as
{{\boldsymbol{E}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}
and
{{\boldsymbol{H}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{E}}}
, respectively. The eccentricity vector
{{\boldsymbol{e}}}
and Hamilton vector
{{\boldsymbol{h}}}
seen in Eq.(
1.4
) are simply a scaling by
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}
, that is,
{{\boldsymbol{e}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\boldsymbol{E}}}
and
{{\boldsymbol{h}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\boldsymbol{H}}}
. We should also not that, in the present context, all of these “vectors” are really just the inertial cartesian components (
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
-valued functions).

Their cartesian components are given in terms of the inertial cartesian coordinates,
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})
, and in terms of the projective coordinates (using Eq.(
1.1
) or Eq.(
1.1
)) as
follows:
23
23

23

It has already been shown that
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
. The expressions for
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}
and
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{h}}}
in terms of
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
are seen as follows using the relations in Eq.(
1.1
) or Eq.(
1.1
):

\qquad\qquad\qquad{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}\,=\ {{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(-u{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,=\,{\ell}^{2}u{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,=\,{\ell}^{2}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{q}}}+w{{\boldsymbol{p}}}

\qquad\qquad\qquad{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{h}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}\,=\,-u{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}++\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,=\,-(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,\simeq\,(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{p}}}-w{{\boldsymbol{q}}}
.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}&=\,{\ell}^{2}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,-\,w{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}&\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,+\,w{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{h}}}=\,-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}&=\,-(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,-\,w{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}&\simeq\,(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,-\,w{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}&=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\hskip 18.49988pt

where “
\simeq
” denotes expressions simplified using
q=1
and
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
.
It is well-known that the above
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
-valued functions are all conserved for pure Kepler dynamics.
Their normalization defines an orthonormal basis,
\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{h}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}=:\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{o}}}}}_{e},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{o}}}}}_{h},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{o}}}}}_{\ell}\}
, often called the
perifocal basis
. For Keplerian motion, this basis is
constant/stationary.
24
24

24

with
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}}
and
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{h}}}}}
defining the orbital plane;
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}}
directed towards periapsis,
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{h}}}}}
directed along the velocity at periapsis (direction tangent to the orbit), and
\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}
directed normal to the orbit plane, completing the right-handed triad. The magnitude
e=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{e}}}|\!|
is the usual dimensionless eccentricity of the orbit.

We may verify this by substituting into the above the simplified Kepler solutions from Eq.(
•
‣
1.4
), leading to:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}_{\tau}&\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}_{\tau}(u_{\tau}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}_{\tau}}){{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\,+\,w_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}&\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}_{0}}){{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,+\,w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{\tau}&\simeq\,(u_{\tau}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}_{\tau}}){{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\,-\,w_{\tau}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}&\simeq\,(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}_{0}}){{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,-\,w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{\tau}&=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}&=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{array}

verifying that
({{\boldsymbol{e}}},{{\boldsymbol{h}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})=({{\boldsymbol{e}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{h}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
are indeed conserved for pure Kepler motion.
Furthermore, if
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
coincides with periapsis such that
\tau
is the actual true anomaly, then
\dot{r}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
and
w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=\acute{u}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-\dot{r}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
vanishes and the first two of the above simplify to the following (with
{\ell}={\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
):

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\text{\scriptsize{if $\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0$}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{is periapsis}}}\end{array}\!\!\!:

\displaystyle w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-\dot{r}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{e}}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}{{\boldsymbol{h}}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}){{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{array}

Note that
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/{\ell}^{2}
, which appears frequently, is the inverse of the well-known
semilatus rectum
,
P_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{slr}}$}}={\ell}^{2}/{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}
. Still assuming
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
coincides with periapsis (i.e.,
r_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{min}}$}}
), then we may use the conic section formula,
r_{\tau}=P_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{slr}}$}}/(1+e\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})
, to obtain:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\text{\scriptsize{if $\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0$}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{is periapsis}}}\end{array}\!\!\!:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}u_{\tau}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{r}-\tfrac{1}{P_{{\mathrm{slr}}}}=\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}e\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&,\hskip 18.49988ptu_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}-\tfrac{1}{P_{{\mathrm{slr}}}}=\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}e\,=\,\tfrac{e}{(1+e)r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{e}{(1+e)}u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}\end{array}

where
e=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{e}}}|\!|
is the classic eccentricity and where
({\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu})_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=({\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu})_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}
corresponds to periapsis (i.e.,
r_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}=r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{min}}$}}=P_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{slr}}$}}/(1+e)
and
u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}=u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{max}}$}}=(1+e)/P_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{slr}}$}}
correspond to the closest approach).
We may also consider the above within specific cases (e.g., elliptic, parabolic, etc.), for which we note:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\text{\scriptsize{if $\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0$}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{is periapsis}}}\end{array}\!\!\!:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlll}{\text{\scriptsize{circular }}}(e=0):&\hskip 9.24994ptu_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=u_{\tau}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\,=\,0\\[4.0pt]
{\text{\scriptsize{elliptic }}}(e<1):&\hskip 9.24994ptu_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\tfrac{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}-r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\tfrac{1}{r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}-\tfrac{1}{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}})\;=\tfrac{1}{2}(u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}-u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}})\\[4.0pt]
{\text{\scriptsize{parabolic }}}(e=1):&\hskip 9.24994ptu_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{2r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}\\[4.0pt]
{\text{\scriptsize{hyperbolic }}}(e>1):&\hskip 9.24994ptu_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}e\,=\,\tfrac{e}{(1+e)}u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{p}}$}}\;=\;\begin{matrix}{\text{\scriptsize{same as above}}}\\[-1.0pt]
{\text{\scriptsize{general relations}}}\end{matrix}\end{array}

The equalities indicated above for the elliptic case (
e<1
) have also used the well-known elliptic relations
P_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{slr}}$}}={\ell}^{2}/{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}=2\tfrac{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}+r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}
and
e=\tfrac{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}-r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}{r_{{\mathrm{a}}}+r_{{\mathrm{p}}}}
, where
r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}=r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{max}}$}}
and
u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}=1/r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}=u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{min}}$}}
corresponds to apoapsis (farthest approach of an elliptic/closed orbit).
Although apoapsis only exists in truth for elliptic/closed orbits, note that the above relations for the elliptic case (
e<1
) also hold in the parabolic case (
e=1
) using the limits
r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}\mapsto\infty
and, thus,
u_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}=1/r_{\scalebox{0.7}{${\mathrm{a}}$}}\mapsto 0
.

2.  NEW ORBIT ELEMENTS FOR ARBITRARILY-PERTURBED KEPLER DYNAMICS

We now pick back up with the closed-form
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized Kepler solutions in projective coordinates given in section
1.4
(also in appendix
B
).
More specifically, solutions in our “
w
-modified” projective coordinates wherein the conjugate pair
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
is replaced by the quasi-conjugate pair
(u,w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
.
We will use the closed-form solutions for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)
to define a set of orbit elements,
({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},U,{{\boldsymbol{P}}},W)
, along with their governing equations of motion, which correspond to constant initial conditions in the unperturbed Kepler case and which are osculating elements in the general perturbed case.

Some of these developments will use the matrix formulation of Kepler dynamics in projective coordinates detailed in appendix
B
.

2.1  Variation of Parameters for “Initial Conditions as Elements”

Before considering the problem at hand (orbital dynamics in projective coordinates), let us first outline the general method of variation of parameters (VOP) for “initial conditions as elements” for any finite-dimensional autonomous ODE on
{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
.

General Method.

Though our notation here may overlap with specific notation used elsewhere in this work, the following general theory is in no way specific to orbital dynamics or projective coordinates. It pertains to any finite-dimensional first order system of autonomous ODEs.
Here, we will use
\mathring{\square}:=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}
where
\tau
is any arbitrary evolution parameter. We also use
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
to denote any arbitrary set of state space coordinates.
The general method is as follows:

1.

Staring with some unperturbed dynamical system, let
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
be an autonomous first-order ODE on
{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
. Assume that this has known closed-form solution,
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
, for arbitrary initial conditions
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
. Here, we may view the vector field simply as a smooth map,
{{\boldsymbol{X}}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}\to{\mathrm{T}}_{\mkern-2.0mu\mkern 2.0mu}\boldsymbol{.}{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}\cong{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
, with
\tau
-parameterized flow
\varphi_{\tau}:{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
and inverse
\varphi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}=\varphi_{-\tau}
which, by definition, satisfies:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial\tau}\varphi_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}\circ{\varphi_{\tau}}

(231)

2.

We now consider the perturbed ODE described by
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
, where
{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}
models the perturbations.

3.

Using the same
un
perturbed flow,
\varphi_{\tau}
, of
{{\boldsymbol{X}}}
, we define new coordinates,
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
, by a
\tau
-dependent transformation:

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}:=\varphi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})\hskip 9.24994pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 9.24994pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})

(232)

4.

We wish to find the governing equations for
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
such that
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{\tau})
is a solution to the perturbed ODE
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
. In the unperturbed case
{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}=0
, then we should get constant solutions
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
. That is,
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
defined by Eq.(
232
) are “slow” coordinates (often called
elements
in the context of celestial mechanics); they evolve slowly in the presence of perturbations, and remain constant for the original, unperturbed, dynamics. In a bit more detail:

•

We wish to obtain some (generally, nonautonomous) ODE
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}={{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})
whose solutions
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{\tau}=\theta_{\tau,\tau_{0}}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
are such that
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{\tau})
is a solution to the perturbed ODE
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
.
Assuming
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
, then define
\theta_{\tau}:=\theta_{\tau,0}
and note that
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=\varphi_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})={{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
such that

{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{\tau})=\varphi_{\tau}\circ\theta_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})=\varphi_{\tau}\circ\theta_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
.
I.e., the flow of the perturbed dynamics,
{{\boldsymbol{X}}}+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}
, is given by
\varphi_{\tau}\circ\theta_{\tau}
, where
\varphi_{\tau}
is the original unperturbed flow of
{{\boldsymbol{X}}}
(and also the map for the coordinate transformation
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\leftrightarrow{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
), and where
\theta_{\tau}=\theta_{\tau,0}
is the flow of the yet-to-be-determined vector field
{{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}
for
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}={{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})
.

5.

Applying the chain rule to
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})
, and making use of the relation
\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial\tau}\varphi_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}\circ{\varphi_{\tau}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{X}}}(\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})

(233)

6.

Equating the above with
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
and solving for
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}
leads to:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})\;\;=\;\;\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{X}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,(\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})\;=\;(\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}(\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))

For our purposes, the partials of
\varphi_{\tau}
are simply the Jacobian of the coordinate transformation in Eq.(
232
). That is,
adopting the common information notation
\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\equiv\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}
and
(\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\equiv(\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\equiv\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\equiv\tfrac{\uppartial(\varphi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}})}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}
— note these Jacobians are
\tau
-dependent — then we may write:

\displaystyle\boxed{\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,(\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt{\text{\footnotesize{i.e.,}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt{{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}=(\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}

(235)

Note we have omitted some compositions; the above should really be
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}=(\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}(\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))=(\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}})\circ\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})
, where the composition ensures that the right-hand-side of the above is expressed in terms of
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
.
A more formal statement of Eq.(
235
) would
be:
25
25

25

Note that Eq.(
6
) is equivalent to:

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,(\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})\;=\;(\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}(\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))\;\;\equiv\,({\textbf{{{d}}}}\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}(\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}))\;=\;({\textbf{{{d}}}}\varphi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}})_{\varphi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})}\;=\;(\varphi_{\tau}^{*}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}})_{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}

(236)

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}={{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{E}}}_{\tau}:=\varphi_{\tau}^{*}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\,=\,({\textbf{{{d}}}}\varphi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}})\circ{\varphi_{\tau}}

(237)

Yet, given the informal notational conventions used throughout this work, the less precise version in Eq.(
235
) will be sufficient for our purposes.

Applied to the Problem at Hand.

Now return to the problem at hand: Kepler dynamics expressed in our “
w
-modified” projective coordinates
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
as detailed in section
1.4
(or, equivalently, in appendix
B
). Recall that
w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
is a quasi-momentum coordinate that we use in place of the conjugate momentum coordinate
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}

(this makes the
first
-order Kepler dynamics fully linear
26
26

26

We saw in section
1
that the
first
-order
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized Kepler dynamics for
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
are fully linear, while those of the true canonical coordinate set
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
are not fully linear due to nonlinearity in the
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
dynamics.
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

For the following, it will be convenient to re-order our phase space coordinates
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)
to the modified order
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
. That is, we split them into a “
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
-part” (rotational motion) and “
(u,w)
-part” (radial motion):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{standard}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{ordering}}}\end{array}\!\!\!:\left.\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)\simeq({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},\acute{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}})\end{array}\right.

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{modified}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{ordering}}}\end{array}\!\!\!:\left.\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)\simeq({{\boldsymbol{q}}},\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}},u,\acute{u})\end{array}\right.

Unless indicated otherwise, any matrices appearing in the following are in relation to the modified ordering
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
.

So far, we have been simultaneously considering the
s
- and
\tau
-parameterized perturbed (or not) Kepler dynamics.
We will now stick with
\tau
as our chosen evolution parameter, where
{\mathrm{d}}t=(r^{2}/{\ell}){\mathrm{d}}\tau=1/({\ell}u^{2}){\mathrm{d}}\tau
, making
\tau
the true anomaly up to an additive constant.
Everything that follows could be also carried out using
s
as the evolution parameter rather than
\tau
(where
{\mathrm{d}}t=r^{2}{\mathrm{d}}s=u^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-2$}}{\mathrm{d}}s
). However, the
\tau
-parameterized Kepler dynamics have unit natural frequency
1
, whereas the
s
-parameterized dynamics have natural frequency
{\ell}
(angular momentum magnitude) which is generally
not
constant in the perturbed case and the simple relation
\tau={\ell}s
does
not
hold. We therefore choose to use
\tau
rather than
s
for simplicity. In particular, to simplify the variation of parameters (VOP)
procedure.
27
27

27

Taking partials w.r.t
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
of combinations of
\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{{\ell}s}
and
\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{{\ell}s}
is more burdensome than taking partials of combinations of
\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}
and
\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}
.

The resulting ODEs can always be reparameterized by an appropriate conformal scaling.

The
\tau
-parameterized perturbed Kepler dynamics are given as a first-order matrix ODE as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)

,

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}={{\boldsymbol{X}}}+{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}M({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}

,

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}=({{\boldsymbol{0}}},\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}},0,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})

,

\displaystyle\mathring{t}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}

(244)

where the term
{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}M({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{k}}}
corresponds to the unperturbed Kepler dynamics — with
M\in\mathfrak{sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{8\times 8}
and
{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
defined in section
B.1
— and where the perturbations,
{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
, are given in terms of the generalized forces
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
as seen above (with
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
defined in Eq.(
1.1
)).

We denote the elements corresponding to initial conditions of
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
by

{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}=({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W)
. That is, the elements
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
are defined from the coordinates
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
using the
\tau
-parameterized Kepler solutions from section
1.4
(equivalently, section
B
). We may, or may not, choose to simplify this transformation using the integrals of motion
q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
.
We will consider first the unsimplified case, then the simplified case.
In
either
case, we note that
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}
is partitioned as:

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}=({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W)\end{array}

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\,=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}})}{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}&\tfrac{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}})}{\uppartial(u,w)}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial(U,W)}{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}&\tfrac{\uppartial(U,W)}{\uppartial(u,w)}\end{pmatrix}$}}\;=\;\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{6\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{6\times 2}\\[14.0pt]
\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{2\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial u}&\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial w}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial u}&\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial w}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{2\times 2}\end{pmatrix}$}}

such that the general VOP formula in Eq.(
235
) leads to:

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{U}=\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial U}{\uppartial w}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{W}=\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial W}{\uppartial w}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}

2.2  Elements from Kepler Solutions

We define elements

{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}=({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W)
from the coordinates
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)

using the unsimplified Kepler flow,
\psi_{\tau}:{\mathbb{R}}^{8}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
with
\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}=\psi_{-\tau}
, from Eq.(
B.1
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\boxed{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=\psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})=\Psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{L}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}(L)\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}:=\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})=\Psi_{-\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{-\tau}({\ell})}\\[10.0pt]
\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u\,=\,(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{L^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{L}W\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{L^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
w\,=\,-L(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{L^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,W\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\,:=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\,:=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
U\,:=\,(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
W\,:=\,{\ell}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+w\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\end{array}

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{L}}}\equiv{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt

where
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
and where we have temporarily introduced
{{\boldsymbol{L}}}={{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}
only to clarify "what is a function of what".
Yet, all angular momentum functions are the same in terms of either
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
or
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
for the following reason. Recall that
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
— as well as
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}|\!|
,
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
, and
|\!|{{\boldsymbol{p}}}|\!|
— are all integrals of motion of the Kepler solution flow,
\psi_{\tau}
. Clearly, the above transformation then also preserves the form of these functions. Indeed, it is quick to verify from the above that:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rclllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}&=&{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}={{\boldsymbol{L}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}&=&{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}={{\boldsymbol{L}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\ell}^{2}=q^{2}p^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})^{2}&=&Q^{2}P^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{Q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}})^{2}=L^{2}\\[4.0pt]
{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&=&{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&=&{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{L}}}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}q^{2}=Q^{2}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}={{\boldsymbol{Q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\\[4.0pt]
p^{2}=P^{2}\\[4.0pt]
\\[4.0pt]
\end{array}

We will now dispense with
{{\boldsymbol{L}}}
, using only
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}
with the understanding that it can always be interpreted as a function of either
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
or
({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}})
as indicated above.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Integrals of motion.

Recall that
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}
and
p=P
are integrals of motion only in the case of pure central-force dynamics, but not in the general perturbed case. However,
recall from Eq.(

■

‣
1.1
) that, for any and all arbitrary forces,
q
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
are
always
integrals of motion. It follows from Eq.(
2.2
) that
Q
and
{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}
are likewise integrals of motion for all arbitrary perturbations.
As previously discussed, we are always free to limit consideration to the values
q=Q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}=0
, and simplify governing equations accordingly. We will make these simplifications later on.

Element ODEs.

The equations of motion for
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}=({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W
) resulting from the VOP method are obtained using the general formula
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}
from Eq.(
235
), with
{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}
given for the problem at hand:

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}=({{\boldsymbol{0}}},\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}},0,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})\,=\,\mathring{t}({{\boldsymbol{0}}},{{\boldsymbol{f}}},0,u^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})

\displaystyle\mathring{t}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\hskip 18.49988pt

(268)

where

1.1
)
and where

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}

\displaystyle\,=\,\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}){\raisebox{0.51663pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\big)\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.51663pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]

\mathring{W}=\mathring{t}\Big(u^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\Big)\end{array}

\displaystyle\;\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}\,=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{Q^{2}}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&,\\[8.0pt]
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}\,=\,\mathring{t}\Big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big[{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\big]\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{U}\,=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\Big(u^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\big[U+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\Big)\\[8.0pt]
\mathring{W}\,=\,\mathring{t}\Big(u^{2}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\Big)\end{array}

where the relations in Eq.(
2.2
) hold.
We may also express the right-hand-side of the ODEs in Eq.(
2.2
) in terms of the three inertial cartesian components,
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}
, of the total perturbing forces. This leads to the following (using Eq.(
•
‣
2.2
)-Eq.(

‣
2.2
) below):

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}&\!\!\!=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{Q}{{\ell}u}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\hskip 9.24994pt=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{Q}{{\ell}u}F_{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}&\!\!\!=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{uq}\Big(({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big[{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\big]\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{U}&\!\!\!=\,\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u}\Big(u{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\big[U+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}})\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u}\Big(F_{r}u\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-F_{\tau}\big[U+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\Big)\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{W}&\!\!\!=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{u}\Big(u{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}})\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{u}\Big(F_{r}u\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-F_{\tau}(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\Big)\end{array}}\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathring{t}=1/({\ell}u^{2})\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt

where the second equalities for
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}
,

, and

follow from the fact that

\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\!{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}=\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{r},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{\tau},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{{\ell}}\}

is the LVLH basis (rather, its inertial cartesian components)
and where we have denoted by
(F_{r},F_{\tau},F_{{\ell}})
the LVLH components of the total perturbing force.

Some generalized force relations.

Recall the generalized forces
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
are given in terms of the inertial cartesian components,
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}
, as in Eq.(
1.1
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{uq}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\;=\,-\tfrac{1}{uq}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\\[4.0pt]
f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}=0\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}=\tfrac{1}{q}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}=\tfrac{1}{uq^{2}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\,\simeq\tfrac{1}{u}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\\[3.0pt]

from which we then note:

Simplified Element ODEs.

As mentioned, we may simplify the dynamics using the integrals of motion
q=Q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}=0
(implying the relations in Eq.(
1.1
)). This means that,
in addition to the relations in Eq.(
2.2
), we now further have that
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
and
({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}})
satisfy:

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{using:}}}\\
q=1\\[1.0pt]
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0\end{array}\Rightarrow\;\;\left\{\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{ll}Q^{2}=q^{2}\simeq 1\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq 0\\[4.0pt]
P^{2}=p^{2}\simeq{\ell}^{2}\end{array}\right.\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rcllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}&,&{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\simeq-{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,&{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\simeq{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&\simeq&{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt

from Eq.(

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt\simeq\,-\mathring{t}\tfrac{1}{Pu}F_{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[6.0pt]
