\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\ell}\simeq P\\[3.0pt]
\mathring{t}\simeq 1/(Pu^{2})\end{array}

where the second equalities follow from the fact that
\{{{\boldsymbol{q}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}\simeq\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{r},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{\tau},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{t}}}}}_{{\ell}}\}

is the LVLH basis (rather, its inertial cartesian components)
and where we have denoted by
(F_{r},F_{\tau},F_{{\ell}})
the LVLH components of the total perturbing force.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Note from Eq.(
2.2
) that if the perturbing force is also some central-force of any kind (i.e., if
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}=F_{r}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}=F_{r}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}
), then
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}=\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}=0
and these elements remain constant (as does
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{P}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}
). Similarly, if
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}=F_{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}
is purely in the orbit normal direction, then
\mathring{U}=\mathring{W}=0
.

Finally, we note that one could always choose to use the time,
t
, as the evolution parameter. The
t
-parameterized element ODEs follow from Eq.(
2.2
) as:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\dot{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,-\tfrac{1}{Pu}F_{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&,\\[6.0pt]
\dot{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}&\!\!\!\simeq\,\tfrac{1}{u}(F_{\tau}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{P}}}}}\,+\,F_{{\ell}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}})&,\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\dot{U}&\!\!\!\simeq\,\tfrac{1}{Pu}\Big(F_{r}u\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-F_{\tau}\big[U+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\Big)\\[6.0pt]
\dot{W}&\!\!\!\simeq\,-\tfrac{1}{u}\Big(F_{r}u\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-F_{\tau}(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\Big)\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\dot{\tau}\,=\,{\ell}u^{2}\simeq Pu^{2}

where
\tau
itself is included as a ninth coordinate in the same way that
t
was previously included as a ninth coordinate (though we have not always been indicating this explicitly).

Elements from Simplified Kepler Solutions.

The above simplified element ODEs in Eq.(
2.2
) and Eq.(
2.2
) are still for
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}:=\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})
defined from
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
using the
unsimplified
Kepler solutions for
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
, as in Eq.(
2.2
). Only the final ODEs for
{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
are simplified, but not their definition. We now simplify their definition to begin with. The resulting ODEs are the same as those already given in Eq.(
2.2
)-Eq.(
2.2
).

Using the
simplified
Kepler flow from Eq.(
B.2
) — that is, simplified using the integrals of motion
q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
, along with the subsequent relations in Eq.(
1.1
) — then the elements

{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}=({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W)
are defined from
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ccccc}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=\phi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{\xi}}})=\Phi_{\tau}({\ell}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}({\ell})\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}:=\phi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})=\Phi_{-\tau}({\ell}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{-\tau}({\ell})\\[10.0pt]
\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,-{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u\,=\,(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}W\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
w\,=\,-{\ell}(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,W\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}:=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{P}}}:=\,{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
U:=\,(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
W:=\,{\ell}(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+w\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\end{array}

where
{\ell}\simeq p=P
. That is, the above definition using the simplified Kepler flow could be written more explicitly as:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{P}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,-P{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
u\,=\,(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{P}W\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}}\\[3.0pt]
w\,=\,-P(U-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{P^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,W\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}:=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{p}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{P}}}:=\,p{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
U:=\,(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{p}w\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p^{2}}\\[3.0pt]
W:=\,p(u-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+w\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}

For the above, the matrix
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}
is given as follows (note this is not the same
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}
as given previously in Eq.(
269
) or Eq.(
270
)):

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\,=\,\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&-\tfrac{1}{p}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.51663pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
p\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.51663pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\\[14.0pt]
\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0&-\tfrac{1}{p}\big[\tfrac{2{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})-\tfrac{1}{p}w\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\\[3.0pt]
0&(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{p^{2}})\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&-\tfrac{1}{p}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[3.0pt]
p\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\end{pmatrix}$}}

(341)

where part of the upper and lower sub-matrices can be rewritten using:

\displaystyle\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}=\tfrac{1}{p}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}+({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\tfrac{2{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\;=\;U+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}-(u+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}

(342)

The governing equations for the elements defined in Eq.(
2.2
) or Eq.(
2.2
) are again found using the VOP equation
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{Y}}}
. With
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}
given as above in Eq.(
341
), this leads to precisely the same simplified ODEs as in Eq.(
2.2
)-Eq.(
2.2
) (after appropriate application of various relations given in section
2.2
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Although we have used the same notation,
({{\boldsymbol{Q}}},{{\boldsymbol{P}}},U,W)
, the elements defined in Eq.(
2.2
) or Eq.(
2.2
) are not necessarily the same as the elements defined from the unsimplified solution as in Eq.(
2.2
). However, when one limits consideration of the integrals of motion
q
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
to the values
q=Q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}=0
(which is the most sensible choice), then these definitions of the elements coincide, as do their governing equations of motion which are given by Eq.(
2.2
).

Elements
\mapsto
Cartesian Coordinates.

The transformation for inertial cartesian coordinates
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})
in terms of the elements follows immediately from the (unsimplified) definition of the elements in Eq.(
2.2
), along with Eq.(
1.4
):

\displaystyle\;\;\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}=\tfrac{1}{u}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\,=\,\dfrac{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{(U-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{1}{{\ell}}W\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\\[4.0pt]
\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}=-u{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-w{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}&\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{V}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}})\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{V}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big[{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{V}}}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)+{\ell}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\end{array}\;\;

with
{{\boldsymbol{V}}}:=-U{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}-W{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}

and where
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}
and
{\ell}^{2}=Q^{2}P^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{Q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}})^{2}
.
The above does not make use of the integrals of motion.
If we simplify the above using the integrals of motion
q=Q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}=0
, then:

\displaystyle\;\;\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\,\simeq\,\tfrac{1}{u}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,\simeq\,\dfrac{{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{P}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}}{(U-\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{1}{{\ell}}W\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\,+\,\frac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,\simeq\,u{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-w{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{V}}}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{{\boldsymbol{P}}}(\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-1)-{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\big]\end{array}\;\;

with
{{\boldsymbol{V}}}\simeq U{{\boldsymbol{P}}}-W{{\boldsymbol{Q}}}

and
{\ell}^{2}\simeq P^{2}
.

2.3  Example:
J2
Gravitational Perturbation

Notation
.

Here, the symbol
J_{2}
is the “
J_{2}
constant" (second zonal harmonic) for the leading order gravitational correction for oblate bodies — a numeric constant depending on shape and mass distribution of the body. It should not be confused with the canonical symplectic
2\raisebox{0.34445pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}2
matrix (also denoted
J_{2}
but not used here).

As an example of the perturbed Kepler problem, consider the two-body problem for masses
m_{a}
and
m_{b}
, where
m_{b}
is a uniform sphere but
m_{a}
is some oblate body with equatorial radius
R_{a}
whose
J_{2}
term is a significant gravitational perturbation. The inertial cartesian coordinate Hamiltonian, with
V^{1}
modeling the
J_{2}
term, is given by

\displaystyle{{K}}=\tfrac{1}{2}{\nu}^{2}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{r}+V^{1}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988ptV^{1}({{\boldsymbol{r}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{3}\tfrac{\tilde{J}_{2}}{r^{3}}(3\hat{r}_{3}^{2}-1)

\displaystyle,\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{J}_{2}:=\tfrac{3}{2}J_{2}{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}R_{a}^{2}\hskip 9.24994pt

(350)

where
\hat{r}_{i}=r_{i}/r
and we have absorbed various numeric constants into
\tilde{J}_{2}
defined above. In the following, we assume no other perturbations are present. That is,
{{\boldsymbol{a}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}}=0
such that
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
, and therefore
({{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}},\alpha_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})=0
such that
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})=(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}},-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial u})
.

There are two approaches we may take when calculating the partials
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial u}
appearing in the equations of motion for the projective coordinates. One method is to first obtain the cartesian components
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
,
which is found from the above as follows:

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\tilde{J}_{2}}{r^{4}}\begin{pmatrix}(5\hat{r}_{3}^{2}-1)\hat{r}_{1}\\[3.0pt]
(5\hat{r}_{3}^{2}-1)\hat{r}_{2}\\[3.0pt]
(5\hat{r}_{3}^{2}-3)\hat{r}_{3}\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\tfrac{\tilde{J}_{2}}{r^{4}}\big((5\hat{r}_{3}^{2}-1){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}-2\hat{r}_{3}\hat{\boldsymbol{{1}}}_{3}\big)\,=\,\tilde{J}_{2}u^{4}\big((5\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-2\hat{q}_{3}\hat{\boldsymbol{{1}}}_{3}\big)\hskip 18.49988pt\;\;\hat{q}_{i}\simeq q_{i}

(351)

where
\hat{\boldsymbol{{1}}}_{3}=(0,0,1)
and
where the form on the right has been expressed in terms of
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u)
using
\hat{r}_{i}=\hat{q}_{i}
and
r=1/u
(it could also be simplified using
q=1
). We may then find
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})=-(\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}},\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial u})

from the above by using Eq.(
1.1
):

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{uq}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial u}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{F}}}

(352)

Alternatively, we may simply use the point transformation,
{{\boldsymbol{r}}}=\tfrac{1}{u}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}
, to express
V^{1}
in terms of
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u)
by using
\hat{r}_{i}=\hat{q}_{i}
and
r=1/u
. Substituting this into
V^{1}({{\boldsymbol{r}}})
given by Eq.(
350
) leads to the projective coordinate Hamiltonian with
V^{1}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})
:

\displaystyle V^{1}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})=\tfrac{1}{3}\tilde{J}_{2}u^{3}(3\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1)\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt

\displaystyle{{H}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}u^{2}\big({\ell}^{2}+u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big)-{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}u+\tfrac{1}{3}\tilde{J}_{2}u^{3}(3\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1)

(353)

Note that we do
not

simplify the potential
V^{1}
using
q=1
as this would change the functional dependence on
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}
and thus lead to an incorrect expression for
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}
. From the above, we find that the correct
J_{2}
generalized forces are:

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{2}{q}\tilde{J}_{2}u^{3}(\hat{q}_{3}^{2}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-\hat{q}_{3}\hat{\boldsymbol{{1}}}_{3})\,\simeq\,2\tilde{J}_{2}u^{3}(q_{3}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}-q_{3}\hat{\boldsymbol{{1}}}_{3})

,

\displaystyle f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{1}}{\uppartial u}\,=\,-\tilde{J}_{2}u^{2}(3\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1)\,\simeq\,-\tilde{J}_{2}u^{2}(3q_{3}^{2}-1)

(354)

where only after differentiating do we simplify the above using
q=1
. It can be verified that substituting Eq.(
351
) into Eq.(
352
) leads to the same result as given above.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Assuming no other perturbations,
the equations of motion for the
J_{2}
-perturbed Kepler problem in projective coordinates, or the associated orbit elements, are obtained simply be substituting Eq.(
354
) for
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
— or Eq.(
351
) for
{{\boldsymbol{F}}}
— wherever these terms appear in the perturbed Kepler dynamics given previously.

Numerical Verification with
J2
Perturbation.

The following plots serve as numerical verification of the dynamics given in
Eq.(
2.2
),
including the
J_{2}
perturbation detailed above.
The following are simulations run for the
J_{2}
-perturbed Kepler problem in the “projective elements” defined in
Eq.(
2.2
),
with initial conditions given in terms of classic orbit elements
(a,e,i,\omega,\Omega,f=\tau)
, with
\tau
the true anomaly, by
a_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=8.59767038{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}10^{3}({\mathrm{km}})
,
e_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0.2
,
i_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=20({\mathrm{deg.}})
,
\omega_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=70({\mathrm{deg.}})
,
\Omega_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=135({\mathrm{deg.}})
, and true anomaly
f_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
. The numerical integration is carried out using ode45 in
matlab
with units scaled such that the Earth’s radius is
R_{\mathrm{e}}=1
, Earth’s gravitational parameter is
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}=1
, and Earths second zonal harmonic is
J_{2}=1.082638{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}10^{-3}
.

J_{2}
gravitational term included in dynamics.)

gravitational term included in dynamics.)

Appx. A
SOME ANGULAR MOMENTUM RELATIONS

We collect some useful relations involving projective coordinates and the angular momentum, which plays a central role throughout this work.
Recall that the angular momentum coordinate vector,
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, the antisymmetric matrix,
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\in\mathfrak{so}^{3}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}
, and the magnitude,
{\ell}
, are given in terms of inertial cartesian coordinates
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{6}
by the usual relations:

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}

,

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}-{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}

,

\displaystyle{\ell}^{2}\,=\,|\!|{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}|\!|\,=\,r^{2}{\nu}^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})^{2}

(355)

For the following, it may be helpful to recall the relations for the Hodge dual on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
given in Eq.(
•
).

General Relations.

The functions in Eq.(
355
) are invariant under family a projective transformations (including that of section
1.1
used in this work, as well as the BF transformation
[
6
]
).
With inertial cartesian coordinates,
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{6}
,
consider a family of “canonically-extended” projective transformations,
{\mathbb{R}}^{8}\ni({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\mapsto({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{6}
, for projective coordinates
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
given as follows for any real numbers
n\neq 0,m\in{\mathbb{R}}

[
11
,
13
]
:
33
33

33

The transformation used in this work corresponds to the case
n=m=-1
.

\displaystyle\;\left.\;\;\begin{array}[]{lllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\,=\,u^{n}q^{m}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{n}q^{m}}\big(({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,+\,\tfrac{u}{nq}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\big)\end{array}\right.

,

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{lllll}\varphi=q-1=0\\[3.0pt]
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}\tfrac{1}{q}up_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}\right)

Only the transformation
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\mapsto({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})
for
{{\boldsymbol{r}}}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
and
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
seen above is needed for the angular momentum relations given below in Eq.(
A
)-Eq.(
A
).
Yet, for completeness, we note that the above is subject to a constraint
\varphi
with associated Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
, and that one may restrict consideration to
\varphi=0
(i.e.,
q=1
) and
\lambda=0
in order to obtain an inverse transformation for
{\mathbb{R}}^{6}\ni({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})\mapsto({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
as follows (see
[
11
]
for details):

\displaystyle\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{array}[]{cc}\text{with:}\\
q=1\\[1.0pt]
\lambda=0\end{array}$}}\;\;\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{lllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988ptu\,=\,r^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1/n$}}\\[3.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,r({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+m{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}+(m+1)({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}){{\boldsymbol{r}}}&,\hskip 18.49988ptp_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,nr^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-1/n$}}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\end{array}\right.

Now, we have introduced the general family of transformations in Eq.(
A
) because, it turns out, the angular momentum functions in Eq.(
A
) are invariant under any such transformation.
It can be verified by direct substitution of
{{\boldsymbol{r}}}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
and
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
from Eq.(
A
) that the functions in Eq.(
A
) are unchanged in the new coordinates:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\\[3.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}-{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\\[3.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{\ell}^{2}=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}|\!|^{2}&\!\!\!=\,r^{2}{\nu}^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})^{2}\\[3.0pt]
&\!\!\!=\,q^{2}p^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})^{2}\end{array}

We further note the following useful relations (where
{\ell}_{ij}=q_{i}p_{j}-p_{i}q_{j}=-{\ell}_{ji}
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{\ell}^{2}\,=\,{\ell}_{i}{\ell}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{2}{\ell}_{ij}{\ell}_{ij}={\ell}_{ij}q_{i}p_{j}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=q^{2}p^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})^{2}\\[4.0pt]
0\,=\,{\ell}_{ij}q_{i}q_{j}={\ell}_{ij}p_{i}p_{j}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}={{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}=-{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=-{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{array}

as well as the following relations for various partial derivatives (where
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{b}}}{{\boldsymbol{a}}}}:={\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{b}}}}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{a}}}}
):

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{rllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,(p^{2}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,(q^{2}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[8.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
-{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
-{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\right|\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\ell}=&{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\ell}=&-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[8.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\ell}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,\tfrac{p^{2}}{{\ell}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\big)&\!\!=\tfrac{p^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
-{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\ell}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})\big)&\!\!=-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
-{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\ell}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\big)\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\ell}=&{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\big)\end{array}

where we have
used
34
34

34

In more detail:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\ell}=&\!\!\!\!{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\big)&=\,\tfrac{p^{2}}{{\ell}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\big)\,=\,\tfrac{p^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
-{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}^{2}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\ell}=&\!\!\!\!{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\big)&=\,-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})\big)\,=\,-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\end{array}

the below relation, following from the fact that
\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}
and
\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}},-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}
are orthonormal bases:

\displaystyle{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}\,=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\,=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}

(389)

For the above angular momentum relations in Eq.(
A
)-Eq.(
389
), we note:

1.

Everything holds exactly the same with
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
replaced by inertial cartesian coordinates
({{\boldsymbol{r}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}})
.

2.

More generally, if for any
{{\boldsymbol{x}}},{{\boldsymbol{y}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
we define
{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}:={{\boldsymbol{x}}}\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{y}}}
(i.e,
{{\boldsymbol{z}}}:={{\boldsymbol{x}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{y}}}={{\boldsymbol{y}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
), then everything holds with
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})
replaced by
({{\boldsymbol{x}}},{{\boldsymbol{y}}},{{\boldsymbol{z}}})
.

3.

Nothing depends on
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
nor the choices of
n\neq 0,m\in{\mathbb{R}}
in the family of transformations in Eq.(
A
).

4.

Nothing involves the functions
\varphi
and
\lambda
in Eq.(
A
). That is, nothing is simplified using
q=1
or
\lambda=0
.

Simplified Relations (in the case
m=-1
).

As mentioned, the above angular momentum relations do not involve the functions
\varphi
or
\lambda
in Eq.(
A
).
Yet, one
could
, if they wished, rewrite some of the above using
\varphi=0
(i.e.,
q=1
) and
\lambda=0
. The reasons for this is the same as mentioned in section
1.1

(reviewed in the footnote
35
35

35

It was shown in
[
11
,
15
]
that
q=|\!|{{\boldsymbol{q}}}|\!|
and
\lambda
are integrals of motion of the Hamiltonian system in any projective coordinates defined by Eq.(
A
) for any
n,m\in{\mathbb{R}}
(note
q
being an integral of motion is equivalent to
\varphi
being an integral of motion). This holds for any arbitrary forces, conservative or nonconservative. Furthermore, we may choose to limit consideration to the values
q=1
and
\lambda=0
as this places no restrictions on the cartesian coordinates. In particular, if Eq.(
A
) is used to transform initial conditions
({{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})\mapsto({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
, then it holds that
q_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=1
and
\lambda_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
and, therefore, it holds for all time along any solution curve in projective coordinates that
q=1
and
\lambda=0
.
).
For the above angular momentum relations, this does not lead to anything particularly interesting until specific values of
n,m\in{\mathbb{R}}
are chosen. In particular, only for the choice
m=-1
does simplifying the above with
q=1
and
\lambda=0
lead to notable changes, detailed below.

This work uses a projective transformation detailed in section
1.1
which corresponds to
m=n=-1
in Eq.(
A
).
36
36

36

That is, we use a transformation given as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}&,\quad(\varphi=q-1=0)\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\,=\,uq({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}&,\quad(\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\end{array}

\displaystyle\longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\,=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}&,\quad u\,=\,1/r\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\,=\,-({{\boldsymbol{r}}}\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\wedge\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}&,\quad p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-r^{2}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\nu}}}}\end{array}

Although the value of
n
is inconsequential for the present discussion, the value of
m
is not: with
m=-1
, it is noteworthy that the multiplier
\lambda
is then given by
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
such that limiting consideration to
\lambda=0
means that
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})
are all mutually orthogonal (this holds only for
m=-1
). Together with
q=1
, this means
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}
and
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
satisfy the below relations (from Eq.(
1.1
)):

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\ell}^{2}=q^{2}p^{2}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})^{2}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}=0\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{using:}}}\\
q=1\\[1.0pt]
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0\end{array}\Rightarrow\;\;\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{ll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\simeq{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\ell}^{2}\simeq p^{2}\end{array}\right.\;\;,\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{lll}{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq-q^{2}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\simeq p^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\simeq{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}\simeq{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt

In particular,
\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}\}\simeq\{{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}}_{r},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}}_{\tau},{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}}_{\ell}\}
are precisely the inertial cartesian components of the LVLH basis.
Using the above relations, the derivatives in Eq.(
A
) simplify to:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}&\simeq\,{\ell}^{2}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\scriptstyle{\frac{{\boldsymbol{1}}}{{\boldsymbol{2}}}}}{\ell}^{2}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}&\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{p}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}&\simeq\,{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}{\ell}\,=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}&\simeq\,{\ell}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}{\ell}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,\tfrac{p^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&\simeq\,{\ell}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&\simeq\,{\ell}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,-\tfrac{q^{2}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&\simeq\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}&\simeq\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,{\text{\footnotesize{Eq.\eqref{angmoment_rels_crd2}}}}&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,{\text{\footnotesize{Eq.\eqref{angmoment_rels_crd2}}}}&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\end{array}

(the last two relations are shown in the footnote
37
37

37

Using the relations in Eq.(
A
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\big)&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\\[4.0pt]
{\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\big({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{{\boldsymbol{p}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}){\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}})\big)&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-{{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}&=\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}-({{\boldsymbol{q}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}){\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&\simeq\,{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\end{array}

).
The above relations denoted with “
\simeq
” follow from using
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
and
q=1
(and thus
{\ell}^{2}\simeq p^{2}
).
These relations are specific to projective coordinates defined using
m=-1
in Eq.(
A
).

We further note that these relations denoted with “
\simeq
” are valid specifically along solution curves of the projective coordinate Hamiltonian system that start with initial conditions satisfying
q_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=1
and
\lambda_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
(we are always free to limit consideration to such initial conditions).

Appx. B
KEPLER DYNAMICS & STATE TRANSITION MATRICES IN PROJECTIVE COORDINATES

The Kepler dynamics and solutions in projective coordinates were given in section
1.4
. We now re-frame the same results with more emphasis on their structure as a single linear matrix ODE and present the corresponding state transition matrix (STM).

Recall for any
n
-dim linear autonomous and
in
homogeneous ODE
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=M{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+{{\boldsymbol{k}}}
(with
M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{{{\textit{{{n}}}}}\times{{\textit{{{n}}}}}}
and
{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{{\textit{{{n}}}}}
constant), the solution may be expressed in terms of initial conditions
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
at
{\varepsilon}=0
as:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\,=\,M{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\,=\,{\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\varepsilon}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}\in\mathrm{Gl}^{{{\textit{{{n}}}}}}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt({\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}={\mathrm{e}}^{-M{\varepsilon}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{{\varepsilon}}:=\int_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{{\varepsilon}}({\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}})^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\end{array}

where
{\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}
is sometimes referred to as the state transition matrix (STM) and is commonly denoted by something like
\Phi_{\varepsilon}
or
\Psi_{\varepsilon}
. Though, it should be noted that
{\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}
is only the true STM for the linear
homogeneous
ODE,
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=M{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
, which has solution
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\varepsilon}}={\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. It should further be noted that the term “state transition matrix” is often used more generally in reference to the matrix which, by abuse of notation, one might write as
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}
. Then, only in the event that
M
and
{{\boldsymbol{k}}}
are truly matrices of real numbers (as opposed to integrals of motion/conserved functions) does it hold that
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}={\mathrm{e}}^{M{\varepsilon}}
.

Returning to the problem at hand,
note the
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized unperturbed
Kepler dynamics from Eq.(
1.4
) can indeed be posed in the above general form of Eq.(
B
), though the matrix that is denoted
M
in Eq.(
B
) will be a matrix of integrals of motion (as opposed to literal real numbers).
We already did as such for the “
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
-part” of the dynamics in Eq.(
1.3
)-Eq.(
138
).
On that note, it will now be convenient to re-order our eight phase space coordinates by splitting them into a “
({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}})
-part” (rotational motion) and “
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
-part” (radial motion), where the latter may also be replaced by the pair
(u,w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
.
That is:

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Coordinate ordering.

Rather than the “standard” configuration-momentum split ordering, we will adopt a modified ordering:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{standard}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{ordering}}}\end{array}\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{z}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})=({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},w)\simeq({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},\acute{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}})\end{array}\right.

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{modified}}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{ordering}}}\end{array}\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{z}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)\simeq({{\boldsymbol{q}}},\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}},u,\acute{u})\end{array}\right.

where, for either ordering,
{{\boldsymbol{z}}}
denotes the full set of “canonical projective coordinates”, while
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
denotes said coordinates with
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
replaced by the quasi-momentum coordinate
w:=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
.

The following developments will mostly use
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
.

Recall that
first
-order Kepler dynamics for
{{\boldsymbol{z}}}
are not fully linear (due to nonlinearity in the
(u,p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
dynamics), but that those of
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
are indeed fully linear. They are fully linear whether or not one simplifies the ODEs using the integrals of motion
q=1
and
\lambda={\hat{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}=0
. We consider first the unsimplified case, then the simplified case.

B.1  Unsimplified Matrix Equations and STM

The ODEs in Eq.(
1.4
) and solutions in Eq.(
1.4
) describe unperturbed (and unsimplified) Kepler dynamics using the coordinates
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
.
Recall these ODEs from Eq.(
1.4
) were given by:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\scriptsize{Kepler-type }}}\\
{\text{\scriptsize{dynamics}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
\acute{u}\,=\,w&,\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{w}=-{\ell}^{2}u+{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\text{\scriptsize{or,}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{u}\,=\,w/{\ell}&,\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{w}=-{\ell}u+{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}/{{\ell}}\end{array}

where
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
is preserved.
The above, and their solutions, are equivalent to the following
(we assume
\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=s_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
):

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}=L{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle{\text{\footnotesize{or,}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}L{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{s}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{s}\end{pmatrix}$}}={\mathrm{e}}^{Ls}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\;\;=\;\;{\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}L\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\\
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}

(436)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u\\
w\end{pmatrix}$}}=\Lambda_{2}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u\\
w\end{pmatrix}$}}+\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{pmatrix}$}}\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle{\text{\footnotesize{or,}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u\\
w\end{pmatrix}$}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\Lambda_{2}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u\\
w\end{pmatrix}$}}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{pmatrix}$}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u_{s}\\
w_{s}\end{pmatrix}$}}={\mathrm{e}}^{\Lambda_{2}s}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\
w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}+\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}^{u}_{s}\\[2.0pt]
{\sigma}^{w}_{s}\end{pmatrix}$}}\;\;=\;\;{\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}\Lambda_{2}\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\\
w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}+\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}^{u}_{\tau}\\[2.0pt]
{\sigma}^{w}_{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}u_{\tau}\\
w_{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}

where
\tau={\ell}s
and where
L=L_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
and
\Lambda_{2}=\Lambda_{2_{0}}
are matrices of integrals of motion that depend on
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
. They are given as follows, along with their matrix exponentials and the inhomogeneous term
{{\boldsymbol{s}}}

(defined as usual
38
38

38

As per the general equations in Eq.(
B
),
({\sigma}^{u},{\sigma}^{w})
is defined by:

\quad\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}^{u}_{s}\\[2.0pt]
{\sigma}^{w}_{s}\end{pmatrix}$}}:=\int_{0}^{s}\big[{\mathrm{e}}^{-\Lambda_{2}s}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{pmatrix}$}}\big]{\mathrm{d}}s\,=\,\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}^{u}_{\tau}\\[2.0pt]
{\sigma}^{w}_{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}:=\int_{0}^{\tau}\big[{\mathrm{e}}^{-\Lambda_{2}\tau/{\ell}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0\\
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}/{\ell}\end{pmatrix}$}}\big]{\mathrm{d}}\tau
.

):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llllll}L:=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}&0\\
0&-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathfrak{sp}^{6}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\cap\mathfrak{so}^{6}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{\mathrm{e}}^{Ls}={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}L\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}R_{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}})&0\\
0&R_{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}})\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathrm{Sp}^{6}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\cap\mathrm{SO}^{6}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988ptR_{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}):={\mathrm{e}}^{-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\tau}\in\mathrm{SO}^{3}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\\[12.0pt]
\Lambda_{2}:=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-{\ell}^{2}&0\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathfrak{sp}^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{\mathrm{e}}^{\Lambda_{2}s}={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}\Lambda_{2}\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\
-{\ell}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathrm{Sp}^{2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\sigma}^{u}_{\tau}\\[2.0pt]
{\sigma}^{w}_{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})\\
\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\end{array}

Note
L=L_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
and
{\mathrm{e}}^{Ls}={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}L\tau}
were already discussed in more detail around Eq.(
136
).
All of the above can be combined as:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)

\displaystyle\boxed{\begin{array}[]{rlll}&\acute{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\,=\,M{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}+{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\\[5.0pt]
{\text{\footnotesize{or,}}}&\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}M{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}\,=\,\Psi_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}}

\displaystyle\tau={\ell}s\hskip 9.24994pt

with
{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
a true numeric constant depending only on
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}
, with
M=M_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8\times 8}
a matrix of integrals of motion, and with
\Psi_{\tau}:={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}M\tau}={\mathrm{e}}^{Ms}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8\times 8}
and
{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
defined as in Eq.(
B
). These are given for the ordering
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)

as
follows:
39
39

39

Using the submatrices defined in Eq.(
B.1
),
M
and
\Psi_{\tau}
are given explicitly by:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}M=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}&0_{3\times 3}\\
0_{3\times 3}&-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}\end{pmatrix}$}}&0_{6\times 2}\\
0_{2\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-{\ell}^{2}&0\end{pmatrix}$}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathfrak{sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\qquad,\qquad\Psi_{\tau}:={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}M\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}R_{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}})&0_{3\times 3}\\
0_{3\times 3}&R_{\tau}({\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}})\end{pmatrix}$}}&0_{6\times 2}\\
0_{2\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\
-{\ell}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}&\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathrm{Sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}M:=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}L&0\\
0&\Lambda_{2}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathfrak{sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt\Psi_{\tau}:={\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}M\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}L\tau}&0\\
0&{\mathrm{e}}^{\frac{1}{{\ell}}\Lambda_{2}\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\in\mathrm{Sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{k}}}:=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
0\\
{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}\end{pmatrix}$}}&,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})\\
\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\end{array}

where
\mathrm{Sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}
and
\mathfrak{sp}^{6;2}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}
denote, respectively, symplectic and Hamiltonian matrices with regards to
J_{6}\oplus J_{2}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}J_{6}&0\\
0&J_{2}\end{pmatrix}$}}
, where
J_{2{{\textit{{{n}}}}}}
denotes the standard symplectic matrix on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2{{\textit{{{n}}}}}}
.
It can be verified that the solution in Eq.(
B.1
), when written out explicitly, agrees with that given previously in Eq.(
1.4
).
The solution is also easily inverted using
\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}=\psi_{-\tau}
:

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{ccccc}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}=\psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})=\Psi_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}+{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau})=\Psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}+{{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}\\[10.0pt]
\begin{array}[]{lllllll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u_{\tau}\,=\,u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}_{0}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}_{0}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})\\[4.0pt]
w_{\tau}\,=\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-{\ell}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}_{0}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}_{\tau}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\\[4.0pt]
u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,u_{\tau}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}_{\tau}}w_{\tau}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}_{\tau}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})\\[4.0pt]
w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,w_{\tau}\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}+{\ell}_{\tau}u_{\tau}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}_{\tau}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{array}\end{array}

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}

where we have defined
\psi_{\tau}:{\mathbb{R}}^{8}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
as the (unsimplified) Kepler flow in coordinates
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}
, and where
{{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}
is defined by

\displaystyle{{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}:=-\Psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}=\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\
\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau})\\
-\tfrac{{\textnormal{{{k}}}}_{1}}{{\ell}}\sin{{\mkern-2.0mu}}{\tau}\end{pmatrix}$}}\,=\,{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{-\tau}

(452)

Note that
\psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}=\psi_{-\tau}
,
\Psi_{\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}=\Psi_{-\tau}
, and
{{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{-\tau}
.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Angular momentum dependence.
Many terms in the above Kepler dynamics depend on the angular momentum,
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
. In particular:

\displaystyle M=M({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\Psi_{\tau}=\Psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}={{\boldsymbol{{\varsigma}}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}})

(453)

with
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}({{\boldsymbol{x}}})={{\boldsymbol{q}}}\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\times\,}$}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
a function of the coordinates.
Yet, since
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}={{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
is conserved for any central-force dynamics, we have treated it as a constant when solving the Kepler ODEs. Still, it should be noted that Eq.(
B.1
) is really a family of linear ODEs parameterized by angular momentum values.
For instance, for a given
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
, it would be more accurate to write the ODE and the solution in Eq.(
B.1
) as:

\displaystyle\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{x}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}_{0}}M({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}_{0}}{{\boldsymbol{k}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}\,=\,\psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})\,=\,\Psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,+\,{{\boldsymbol{{\sigma}}}}_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})

,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}\neq\Psi_{\tau}

(454)

This dependence of
\psi_{\tau}
on
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}
is reflected in the way we have written the explicit solutions for
{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)
in Eq.(
B.1
).

Kepler STM from Unsimplified Solutions.

As indicated above, the matrices
\Psi_{\tau}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}
— both of which are sometimes referred to as the STM — do
not
coincide. When perturbations are considered and
{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}
is no longer conserved, then the object of interest is the more general version of the STM given by
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}
(one might also write this as
{\textbf{{{d}}}}\psi_{\tau}({{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
), which we note is partitioned as:

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}{{\boldsymbol{x}}}=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}},u,w)\end{array}

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}\,=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau})}{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0})}&\tfrac{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau})}{\uppartial(u_{0},w_{0})}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial(u_{\tau},w_{\tau})}{\uppartial({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0})}&\tfrac{\uppartial(u_{\tau},w_{\tau})}{\uppartial(u_{0},w_{0})}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0}}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{6\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\\[4.0pt]
{{\boldsymbol{0}}}&{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{6\times 2}\\[14.0pt]
\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial u_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial u_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial w_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial w_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{0}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{2\times 6}&\text{\footnotesize{$\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial u_{\tau}}{\uppartial u_{0}}&\tfrac{\uppartial u_{\tau}}{\uppartial w_{0}}\\[4.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial w_{\tau}}{\uppartial u_{0}}&\tfrac{\uppartial w_{\tau}}{\uppartial w_{0}}\end{pmatrix}$}}_{2\times 2}\end{pmatrix}$}}

where
(u_{\tau},w_{\tau})
depend on
({{\boldsymbol{q}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{{\boldsymbol{p}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
only through
{\ell}
.
From the Kepler solutions in Eq.(
B.1
) — along with the angular momentum relations in Eq.(
A
)-Eq.(
A
) of appendix
A
— this leads to:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{\tau}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{0}}
