Appx. D
REPARAMETERIZED DYNAMICS AS A HAMILTONIAN SYSTEM ON EXTENDED PHASE SPACE

The following is an extension of section
1.2
. While not critical for the main purposes of this work, we include it here for completeness and to clarify any confusion.

For the “canonical projective coordinates”,
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})=({{\boldsymbol{q}}},u,{{\boldsymbol{p}}},p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
, we mentioned that the
t
-parameterized ODEs in Eq.(
1.1
) are a Hamiltonian system (with nonconservative perturbations). However, the
s
- or
\tau
-parameterized
ODEs in Eq.(
1.2
) or Eq.(
1.2
) are
not
true Hamiltonian systems (with nonconservative perturbations). Recall these were given by:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\acute{t}=\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}\;\;,\hskip 9.24994pt\left.\begin{array}[]{lll}\acute{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}=\acute{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}=\acute{t}(-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}})\end{array}\right\}\hskip 9.24994pt\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\acute{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lllll}\acute{u}\,=\,u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u}\big({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)-\acute{t}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{t}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\;\;,\hskip 9.24994pt\left.\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}=\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}=\mathring{t}(-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}})\end{array}\right\}\hskip 9.24994pt\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{u}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u}\big({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)-\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\mathring{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\end{array}

The above are
conformally
Hamiltonian systems — with possibly-nonconservative perturbations
({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
as described in Eq.(
1.1
) — for the conformal scaling factors
\acute{t}=1/u^{2}
and
\mathring{t}=1/({\ell}u^{2})
, respectively. Even if we remove the nonconservative perturbations, the above will not have all the usual properties of Hamiltonian systems. E.g., volume preservation (Liouville’s theorem) is generally not satisfied and the state transition matrix will generally not be a symplectic matrix. However, there is a way to recover all the usual Hamiltonian properties.

Recovering Hamiltonian Structure Using Extended Phase Space.

The above reparameterized dynamics may indeed be realized as a Hamiltonian system (with nonconservative perturbations) by making use of the
extended phase space
, where
t
is added as configuration coordinate with conjugate momenta coordinate denoted
p_{t}
(see
[
11
,
12
,
16
,
17
]
).
The extended Hamiltonian
\widetilde{{H}}({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}},t,p_{t})
for
s
as the evolution parameter is then defined as
\widetilde{{H}}:=\acute{t}({{H}}+p_{t})
, where
\acute{t}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}s}=1/u^{2}
.
The
s
-parameterized Hamiltonian dynamics are given in projective coordinates using Hamilton’s canonical equations with
\widetilde{{H}}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{{H}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{2}\big({\ell}^{2}+u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)\,+\,\widetilde{V}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,+\,u^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-2$}}p_{t}\phantom{\hskip 18.49988pt-\tfrac{1}{u}\big({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}\big)-\acute{t}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{u}=\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}\,=\,u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{t}=\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}\,=\,1/u^{2}\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\acute{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}+\acute{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&\!\!\!=\,-{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\acute{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial u}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}&\!\!\!=\,-up_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2}-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{V}^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\tfrac{2}{u^{3}}p_{t}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}&=-\tfrac{1}{u}({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2})-\acute{t}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\tfrac{2}{u}\widetilde{{H}}+\acute{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\acute{p}_{t}=-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial t}+\acute{t}f_{t}&\!\!\!=\,-\acute{t}({\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}V^{1}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}})\end{array}

where
\widetilde{V}^{0}:=\acute{t}V^{0}=u^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-2$}}V^{0}
still depends only on
u
, where
V^{1}
is a potential accounting for conservative perturbations, and where
{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}}=({{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}},\alpha_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
account for nonconservative perturbations (all perturbations are combined together in
{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}}=({{\boldsymbol{f}}},f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}})
).
Note that only the above
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
equation differs from the
s
-parameterized conformally-Hamiltonian dynamics in Eq.(
D
) (because
\acute{t}=u^{\scalebox{0.7}{$-2$}}
depends only on
u
).

We may also consider the
\tau
-parameterized Hamiltonian dynamics on extended phase space. These are obtained in a similar manner as Eq.(
D
), but using an extended Hamiltonian
\widehat{{H}}:=\mathring{t}({{H}}+p_{t})=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\widetilde{{H}}
, where
\mathring{t}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}\tau}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}
. This leads to the following Hamiltonian dynamics:

\displaystyle\widehat{{H}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\widetilde{{H}}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-(1-\tfrac{\widetilde{{H}}}{{\ell}^{2}}){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{u}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{t}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\end{array}\;\;,\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{rlllll}\mathring{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}+\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}&\!\!\!=\,-(1-\tfrac{\widetilde{{H}}}{{\ell}^{2}}){\hat{{{\boldsymbol{{\ell}}}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\star}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}+\mathring{t}{{\boldsymbol{f}}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial u}+\mathring{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}&\!\!\!=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u}({\ell}^{2}+2u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}^{2})-\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{0}}{\uppartial u}+\tfrac{2}{u}\widehat{{H}}+\mathring{t}f_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{t}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial t}+\mathring{t}f_{t}&\!\!\!=\,-\mathring{t}({\rotatebox[origin]{15.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}V^{1}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}})\end{array}

which differ notably from the
\tau
-parameterized conformally-Hamiltonian dynamics given in Eq.(
D
).
Yet, somehow, they must be equivalent. Indeed they are, as described below.

It can be shown (cf.
[
16
,
17
]
) that, along a solution curve in extended phase space,
p_{t}
always has a
value
of
p_{t}=-{{H}}
such that the extended Hamiltonians have values
\widetilde{{H}}=0=\widehat{{H}}
(when evaluated along a solution curve).
This relation, when substituted back into the above extended phase space dynamics, lead to ODEs for
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
that are equivalent to the conformal scaling by
\acute{t}
or
\mathring{t}
given in Eq.(
D
) or Eq.(
D
).
For instance, for the
\tau
-parameterized dynamics in Eq.(
D
), we note:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}&\!\!=\;\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}+({{H}}+p_{t})\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}\mathring{t}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}+\mathring{t}{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}}&\!\!=\;\mathring{t}(-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}})-({{H}}+p_{t})\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}\mathring{t}\end{array}

,

\displaystyle p_{t}=-{{H}}\hskip 9.24994pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}\,=\,\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}&=\;\mathring{t}\dot{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}\,=\,\mathring{t}(-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}}}+{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{f}}}}})&=\;\mathring{t}\dot{{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}}}\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt

and similarly for the
s
-parameterized dynamics.
That is, using
p_{t}=-{{H}}
is equivalent to
\widetilde{{H}}=0=\widehat{{H}}
in the ODEs of Eq.(
D
) and Eq.(
D
). The result is equivalent to the conformally-Hamiltonian dynamics given in Eq.(
D
) or Eq.(
D
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

To clarify, the
10
ODEs in Eq.(
D
) and Eq.(
D
) are true Hamiltonian systems (with nonconservative perturbations), whereas the
8
ODEs in Eq.(
D
) or Eq.(
D
) are
not
; they are conformally Hamiltonian systems (with nonconservative perturbations). The ODEs and solution curves for
({\bar{{{\boldsymbol{q}}}}},{\bar{{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}})
agree using
p_{t}=-{{H}}
.
